- `reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/modelCapabilities.ts:138` — the `ltx-2.3-fast` model maps to `defaultWorkerModelName: 'ltx2_22B_distilled'` (the old one), not `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1`
- `ltx-2.3` (non-fast) maps to `ltx2_22B` — also unchanged

> AGENT

Yes — it's a small code change, but there's one gotcha.

**The change itself** — two lines:

- `reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/modelCapabilities.ts:138` — `'ltx2_22B_distilled'` → `'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1'`
- `reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/generation.ts:44` — same

The configs are drop-in compatible: same architecture (`ltx2_22B`), same `ltx2_pipeline: distilled`, same step/frame shapes. The worker routes on `"ltx2" in model_name` so it just works. The 1.1 config even has nicer `preload_URLs` (explicit IC-LoRA union + outpaint) instead of the old `"preload_URLs": "ltx2_22B"` reference.

**The gotcha** — `resolveSelectedModelFromModelName` (line 181) does an exact match on `defaultWorkerModelName`. Any existing generations in the DB with `model_name = 'ltx2_22B_distilled'` would no longer resolve → silently falls back to `wan-2.2` when you reopen them. Three options:

1. Add `ltx2_22B_distilled` as an alias that resolves to `ltx-2.3-fast` (keep history working, new tasks use 1.1)
2. Add a new model entry `ltx-2.3-fast-1.1` instead of replacing — user picks explicitly
3. Replace and accept stale-row fallback (fine if there aren't many old tasks)

**Other costs** — first run per worker downloads ~22B of new weights (~25GB bf16 or ~13GB int8). IC-LoRA union is already downloaded. The old checkpoint stays on disk unless you manually delete it.

Plus a handful of test fixtures to update (7 files hardcode the old key).

Which of the three swap strategies do you want?

> AGENT

In plain terms:

**Right now:** when you pick "LTX-2.3 fast" in the app, the worker loads the old distilled checkpoint.

**What we'd do:** point that same button at the new 1.1 checkpoint file instead. Two lines of code. The app still says "LTX-2.3 fast"; behind the scenes it just uses a better weights file.

**First time you run it:** the worker downloads the new weights (~13-25GB) once, then it's cached.

**The one catch:** any video you generated *before* the swap has the old model's name saved in the database. If you reopen one of those old generations, the app won't recognize the name and will fall back to showing "Wan 2.2" as the selected model. Fix is a one-line alias so old names still map to the "LTX-2.3 fast" button.

40	def _has_payload_value(value: Any) -> bool:
41	    """Return True when a payload field should count as "present"."""
42	    if value is None or value is False:
43	        return False
44	    if isinstance(value, (list, dict, str)):
45	        return bool(value)
46	    return True

49	def _infer_allowed_kinds(model_name: str) -> set[str]:
50	    """Infer which travel guidance kinds are allowed for a model."""
51	    lowered = (model_name or "").lower()
52	
53	    if "ltx2" in lowered:
54	        pipeline_kind = None
55	        try:
56	            model_def = get_model_def(model_name)
57	            if isinstance(model_def, dict):
58	                pipeline_kind = model_def.get("ltx2_pipeline")
59	        except (ImportError, RuntimeError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError, AttributeError):
60	            pipeline_kind = None
61	
62	        is_distilled = pipeline_kind == "distilled" or (
63	            pipeline_kind is None and "distilled" in lowered
64	        )
65	        if is_distilled:
66	            return {"none", "ltx_control", "ltx_hybrid", "ltx_anchor", "uni3c"}
67	        return {"none"}
68	
69	    return {"none", "vace", "uni3c"}

**Summary:**
- `modelCapabilities.ts` — "LTX-2.3 fast" now points at `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1`; added an alias so the old `ltx2_22B_distilled` in existing DB rows still resolves to the "fast" UI button
- `ai-timeline-agent/tools/generation.ts` — AI agent generation tool uses the new key too
- `settings.test.ts` — pinned both the new key and the legacy alias

**What you'll see:**
- Next "LTX-2.3 fast" generation: worker downloads the 1.1 checkpoint (~13–25GB) once, then uses it going forward
- Old generations in your gallery still open with the right model selected
- The Supabase edge function change (`generation.ts`) needs `npx supabase functions deploy ai-timeline-agent --project-ref wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc` to take effect in prod — the frontend change ships on next deploy

1	"""CLI entrypoint for the live worker harness."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import argparse
6	
7	from scripts.live_test import config
8	from scripts.live_test.variant_fresh import run as run_variant_fresh
9	from scripts.live_test.variant_update import run as run_variant_update
10	
11	
12	def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
13	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Run the Reigh live worker harness.")
14	    parser.add_argument("--variant", choices=("fresh", "update"), required=True)
15	    parser.add_argument("--pod-id", help="Existing RunPod pod ID for update-mode takeover.")
16	    parser.add_argument(
17	        "--spawn-takeover",
18	        action="store_true",
19	        help="Spawn a fresh orchestrator-managed pod, then take it over with the local worker branch.",
20	    )
21	    termination_group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()
22	    termination_group.add_argument("--no-terminate", dest="no_terminate", action="store_true")
23	    termination_group.add_argument("--terminate", dest="no_terminate", action="store_false")
24	    parser.set_defaults(no_terminate=None)
25	    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true")
26	    parser.add_argument("--ref", default="main", help="Branch/ref to clone for Variant Fresh.")
27	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-profile", type=int, default=3)
28	    parser.add_argument("--timeout-image", type=int, default=config.TIMEOUT_IMAGE_SEC)
29	    parser.add_argument(
30	        "--timeout-travel-segment",
31	        type=int,
32	        default=config.TIMEOUT_INDIVIDUAL_TRAVEL_SEGMENT_SEC,
33	    )
34	    parser.add_argument(
35	        "--timeout-travel-orchestrator",
36	        type=int,
37	        default=config.TIMEOUT_TRAVEL_ORCHESTRATOR_SEC,
38	    )
39	    parser.add_argument("--anchor-image-a", default=config.ANCHOR_IMAGE_A_URL)
40	    parser.add_argument("--anchor-image-b", default=config.ANCHOR_IMAGE_B_URL)
41	    parser.add_argument(
42	        "--serial",
43	        action="store_true",
44	        help="Reserved for future matrix fan-out; current harness always runs serially.",
45	    )
46	    return parser
47	
48	
49	def _finalize_args(args: argparse.Namespace, parser: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> argparse.Namespace:
50	    if args.variant == "fresh":
51	        if args.pod_id or args.spawn_takeover:
52	            parser.error("--pod-id/--spawn-takeover are only valid with --variant update")
53	        if args.no_terminate is None:
54	            args.no_terminate = False
55	        return args
56	
57	    if bool(args.pod_id) == bool(args.spawn_takeover):
58	        parser.error("update variant requires exactly one of --pod-id or --spawn-takeover")
59	    if args.no_terminate is None:
60	        args.no_terminate = True
61	    return args
62	
63	
64	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
65	    parser = build_parser()
66	    args = _finalize_args(parser.parse_args(argv), parser)
67	    if args.variant == "fresh":
68	        return run_variant_fresh(args)
69	    return run_variant_update(args)
70	
71	
72	if __name__ == "__main__":
73	    raise SystemExit(main())
74
```

```
1	# Worker Matrix Runner
2	
3	`scripts/run_worker_matrix.py` runs a curated set of worker-facing smoke cases and writes artifacts for each case under `artifacts/worker-matrix/`.
4	
5	## What It Covers
6	
7	- Real `worker.py -> process_single_task()` execution with real task routing and parameter conversion
8	- For direct-queue generation cases, a fake immediate queue captures the submitted generation task after real payload conversion, then returns a synthetic output. This catches routing and parameter-shape regressions without requiring GPU execution.
9	- Local-only handler cases (e.g. frame extraction, visualization) can also be added and will run end-to-end without a fake queue.
10	- Per-case logs, inputs, outputs, and summaries
11	
12	## Basic Usage
13	
14	Run the default suite:
15	
16	```bash
17	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py
18	```
19	
20	List available cases:
21	
22	```bash
23	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --list-cases
24	```
25	
26	Run only selected cases:
27	
28	```bash
29	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --case-id qwen_image_style_db_task --case-id z_image_turbo_db_task
30	```
31	
32	Filter by tag, task type, or family:
33	
34	```bash
35	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --tag qwen
36	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --task-type extract_frame
37	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --family direct_queue
38	```
39	
40	Rerun only failed cases from a previous summary:
41	
42	```bash
43	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --failed-from artifacts/worker-matrix/<run>/summary.json
44	```
45	
46	Stop on first failure:
47	
48	```bash
49	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --stop-on-failure
50	```
51	
52	Include non-default cases:
53	
54	```bash
55	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --all-cases
56	```
57	
58	Override manifest or output directory:
59	
60	```bash
61	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --manifest path/to/custom.json --output-dir path/to/output
62	```
63	
64	## Real Mode (GPU Generation)
65	
66	Pass `--real` to use the real `HeadlessTaskQueue` and GPU pipeline instead of the fake queue. This runs the full generation path — model loading, inference, output writing — without touching the production database.
67	
68	```bash
69	# Fastest smoke test — z_image is the smallest model
70	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --real --case-id z_image_turbo_db_task
71	
72	# With model preloading to avoid cold-start on first case
73	python scripts/run_worker_matrix.py --real --preload-model z_image --case-id z_image_turbo_db_task
74	```
75	
76	Requirements:
77	- CUDA-capable GPU with `torch.cuda.is_available() == True`
78	- Model weights accessible from the `Wan2GP/` directory
79	
80	Generated outputs land in each case's `outputs/` directory (e.g. `artifacts/worker-matrix/<run>/z_image_turbo_db_task/outputs/`).
```

30	def _deep_merge(base: dict[str, Any], overrides: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:

> AGENT

Yes — there are two testing systems, and one of them is fully live/pod-spinning:

**1. `scripts/live_test/` — full pod-spinning harness** (`python -m scripts.live_test.main --variant fresh|update`)
- **`fresh`**: spins up a brand-new RunPod pod, clones Reigh-Worker from GitHub, installs, boots the worker, runs a task matrix, fetches logs, and terminates the pod
- **`update`**: takes over an existing pod (or spawns one via the orchestrator) and overlays your local worker branch on top — useful for iterating without full fresh install
- Runs a real end-to-end matrix: WAN travel, LTX travel, image gen — spoofs tasks into the real DB and polls for completion
- Requires `RUNPOD_API_KEY`, `REIGH_LIVE_TEST_TOKEN`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`
- **Caveat:** this whole directory is currently **untracked** in reigh-worker git — it exists on your disk but has never been committed. So it's your local WIP harness, not shipped to main.

**2. `scripts/run_worker_matrix.py` — committed smoke matrix**
- Runs `worker.py -> process_single_task()` locally with fake queue for payload/routing regression (no GPU needed)
- `--real` flag switches to real `HeadlessTaskQueue` + GPU pipeline if you have a local CUDA GPU
- This one's tracked and shipped; docs at `docs/worker-matrix-runner.md`

**Relevant to what we just shipped:** `scripts/live_test/config.py:126` hardcodes `LTX_MODEL_ID = "ltx2_22B_distilled"` — the **old** key. If you want the live harness to exercise the 1.1 checkpoint we just shipped, that constant needs flipping to `"ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1"`. Since the dir is uncommitted anyway, I can change it locally — but it won't reach anyone else until the harness gets committed.

Want me to update that constant?

1	"""Token resolution helpers for the live-test harness."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	
6	class TokenResolutionError(RuntimeError):
7	    """Raised when a PAT cannot be resolved to exactly one user."""
8	
9	
10	def resolve_token_to_user_id(db, token: str) -> str:
11	    """Resolve a PAT to the owning user ID and require a single match."""
12	    token_value = str(token).strip()
13	    if not token_value:
14	        raise TokenResolutionError("Token is empty")
15	
16	    result = (
17	        db.supabase.table("user_api_tokens")
18	        .select("user_id")
19	        .eq("token", token_value)
20	        .execute()
21	    )
22	    rows = list(result.data or [])
23	    if len(rows) != 1:
24	        raise TokenResolutionError(
25	            f"Expected exactly one user_api_tokens row for the provided token, found {len(rows)}"
26	        )
27	
28	    user_id = rows[0].get("user_id")
29	    if not user_id:
30	        raise TokenResolutionError("Matched token row did not include user_id")
31	    return str(user_id)
32	
33	
34	__all__ = ["TokenResolutionError", "resolve_token_to_user_id"]
35
```

> AGENT

Yes — it's a **4090**, and it defers to your existing RunPod orchestrator, not a separate config:

- Line 90: fallback is `"NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090"` if the orchestrator import fails
- Lines 73–87: by default it imports `gpu_orchestrator.runpod.client.RunpodClient` and inherits whatever that client is already configured with — so it uses the same GPU type / image / template / storage volumes / RAM tiers the orchestrator uses
- Storage volumes in the fallback are named `Peter, EU-NO-1, EU-CZ-1, EUR-IS-1` — which matches the orchestrator's pool, suggesting the harness is built to piggyback on infra you already have

So a 4090 run is cheap (roughly $0.30–$0.70/hr on RunPod spot/community pricing), and the harness is not its own infra — it's just the orchestrator you already use, driven in "live-test" mode.

1	-- Simplify user_api_tokens table by removing JWT-related fields
2	-- Drop JWT-related columns and update the token column to store simple 24-character tokens
3	
4	BEGIN;
5	
6	-- Remove JWT-related columns and last_used (as requested) - only if table exists
7	DO $$
8	BEGIN
9	  IF EXISTS (SELECT FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_name = 'user_api_tokens') THEN
10	    ALTER TABLE public.user_api_tokens 
11	    DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS jti_hash,
12	    DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS expires_at,
13	    DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS last_used;
14	    
15	    -- Update the token column to be NOT NULL since we'll store the actual token there
16	    ALTER TABLE public.user_api_tokens 
17	    ALTER COLUMN token SET NOT NULL;
18	  END IF;
19	END $$;
20	
21	-- Drop the old index on jti_hash
22	DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_user_api_tokens_jti_hash;
23	DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_user_api_tokens_expires_at;
24	
25	-- Add a unique index on the token column for fast lookups - only if table exists
26	DO $$
27	BEGIN
28	  IF EXISTS (SELECT FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_name = 'user_api_tokens') THEN
29	    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_user_api_tokens_token ON public.user_api_tokens(token);
30	  END IF;
31	END $$;
32	
33	-- Drop the old function first
34	DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS public.verify_api_token(text);
35	
36	-- Create the new verify_api_token function to work with simple tokens - only if table exists
37	DO $$
38	BEGIN
39	  IF EXISTS (SELECT FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_name = 'user_api_tokens') THEN
40	    CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.verify_api_token(p_token text)
41	    RETURNS boolean AS $FUNC$
42	    DECLARE
43	      v_exists boolean;
44	    BEGIN
45	      -- Check if token exists
46	      SELECT EXISTS(
47	        SELECT 1 
48	        FROM public.user_api_tokens
49	        WHERE token = p_token
50	      ) INTO v_exists;
51	      
52	      RETURN v_exists;
53	    END;
54	    $FUNC$ LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER;
55	  END IF;
56	END $$;
57	
58	-- Grant execute permission to authenticated users - only if function exists
59	DO $$
60	BEGIN
61	  IF EXISTS (SELECT FROM information_schema.routines WHERE routine_name = 'verify_api_token') THEN
62	    GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.verify_api_token(text) TO authenticated;
63	  END IF;
64	END $$;
65	
66	COMMIT;
```

> AGENT

In plain terms:

**`REIGH_LIVE_TEST_TOKEN` is a Personal Access Token (PAT) for your own Reigh account** — a 24-character string stored as a row in the Supabase `user_api_tokens` table.

What the harness does with it:
1. Looks up the token in `user_api_tokens` (via service-role Supabase client)
2. Gets back the `user_id` of whoever owns that token
3. Uses that user_id to scope all the test tasks — creates a "live-test" project under your account, inserts spoofed tasks as if that user submitted them, polls for completion

**Why it exists:** the worker pulls tasks from Supabase like any real worker. So the harness has to submit tasks as an actual user. Rather than hardcode a user_id, it uses a token → user mapping so whoever runs the harness tests against their own account.

**Where you'd get one:** from the Reigh app — there's a UI for creating API tokens (same ones used to authenticate external API calls). They're listed under your account settings. If you've never made one, create one there, copy it, export it, run the harness.

Not a secret for the worker itself, not a RunPod thing, not a Supabase service key — just your user's API PAT. If you already use the Reigh API from anywhere (CLI, external script, curl), you probably have one lying around already.

60	def clone_repo_into(ssh, workdir: str, repo_url: str, branch: str) -> None:
61	    parent = workdir.rsplit("/", 1)[0] or "/"
62	    command = (
63	        f"mkdir -p {_quote(parent)} && "
64	        f"test ! -e {_quote(workdir)} && "
65	        f"git clone --branch {_quote(branch)} --single-branch {_quote(repo_url)} {_quote(workdir)}"
66	    )
67	    _execute(ssh, command, timeout=1800)

70	def run_install(ssh, workdir: str) -> None:
71	    package_list = " ".join(APT_INSTALL_PACKAGES)
72	    command = (
73	        "bash -lc "

75	            "set -euo pipefail\n"

79	            "  curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh\n"
80	            "  export PATH=\"$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH\"\n"

> AGENT

Found the bug — `create_pod_and_wait` doesn't actually wait (line 98: `del wait_timeout`), and `open_session` immediately asks for SSH port before the pod's network is up. Patching `open_session` to poll.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/ssh_bootstrap.py",
  "old_string": "def open_session(pod_id: str, api_key: str):\n    ssh_details = live_test_pkg.get_pod_ssh_details(pod_id, api_key)\n    if not ssh_details:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"Could not resolve SSH details for pod {pod_id}\")\n\n    ssh = live_test_pkg.SSHClient(\n        hostname=str(ssh_details[\"ip\"]),\n        port=int(ssh_details[\"port\"]),\n        username=\"root\",\n        [REDACTED](\"password\"),\n    )\n    ssh.connect()\n    return ssh",
  "new_string": "def open_session(pod_id: str, api_key: str, *, ssh_wait_timeout: int = 300, poll_interval: int = 5):\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + ssh_wait_timeout\n    ssh_details = None\n    while time.monotonic() < deadline:\n        ssh_details = live_test_pkg.get_pod_ssh_details(pod_id, api_key)\n        if ssh_details and ssh_details.get(\"ip\") and ssh_details.get(\"port\"):\n            break\n        time.sleep(poll_interval)\n    if not ssh_details or not ssh_details.get(\"ip\") or not ssh_details.get(\"port\"):\n        raise RuntimeError(\n            f\"Could not resolve SSH details for pod {pod_id} within {ssh_wait_timeout}s\"\n        )\n\n    ssh = live_test_pkg.SSHClient(\n        hostname=str(ssh_details[\"ip\"]),\n        port=int(ssh_details[\"port\"]),\n        username=\"root\",\n        [REDACTED](\"password\"),\n    )\n    connect_deadline = time.monotonic() + ssh_wait_timeout\n    last_err: Exception | None = None\n    while time.monotonic() < connect_deadline:\n        try:\n            ssh.connect()\n            return ssh\n        except Exception as exc:\n            last_err = exc\n            time.sleep(poll_interval)\n    raise RuntimeError(f\"Could not SSH into pod {pod_id} within {ssh_wait_timeout}s: {last_err}\")"
}

- ~4 retries to get SSH port info from RunPod API (took ~30s)

**To tail live:** `tail -f /tmp/reigh-live-test/run.log`

The harness is verbose but grep-friendly. Major milestones to watch for:
- `clone_repo_into` — SSH worked, cloning Reigh-Worker
- `run_install` — installing deps (longest step, ~10–15 min)
- `launch_worker_detached` — worker process spawned
- `wait_until_ready` — waiting for worker heartbeat in DB
- `run_matrix` — tasks dispatching (this is where LTX 1.1 actually runs)
- `Pod terminated` — cleanup on finish

I'll check back in ~4 min per the wake-up.

> AGENT

Still alive, 5 min in. SSH handshake keeps failing with "banner errors" / "connection reset" — sshd isn't completing its handshake. My retry loop is working (5-6 attempts so far), but each paramiko attempt takes 20-90s to fail, so we've only burned ~2.5 min of the 5-min connect budget.

Two outcomes likely from here:
- **Good:** sshd finishes booting, one of the next retries succeeds → install begins
- **Bad:** the pod image is broken or we hit a bad machine → 5-min connect budget exhausts, harness terminates pod cleanly via `guarded_terminate`

This is longer than normal cold-boot. Possibilities: (1) heavy image + slow machine, (2) a specific bad instance, or (3) RunPod is exposing port 22 before sshd is ready. I'll check again shortly.

> AGENT

Short answer: yes, it's running my patched harness — but I just spotted a bug that would make the actual LTX 1.1 test meaningless. Let me explain.

**What it's running:**
- ✅ My patched `scripts/live_test/ssh_bootstrap.py` (ed25519 key auth + retry loop + build-essential/python3.11-dev)
- ✅ My patched `scripts/live_test/config.py` with `LTX_MODEL_ID = "ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1"` — so it will submit tasks with the new model key
- ✅ The pod clones `main` from github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker — that's where the LTX 1.1 defaults config lives in `Wan2GP/` (a submodule)

**But:** line 92 of ssh_bootstrap.py does `git clone --branch main --single-branch` — **no `--recurse-submodules`**. So the pod gets the reigh-worker code but `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json` **won't exist on disk**, and the worker will fail to find the model def when it tries to run the LTX case.

Let me kill this run and patch before we waste more pod time.

> TOOL

  "description": "Kill harness and terminate in-flight pod"
}

1	"""Launch-command builders for live worker tests."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import shlex
6	from pathlib import PurePosixPath
7	
8	
9	def _quote(value: str) -> str:
10	    return shlex.quote(value)
11	
12	
13	def _normalize_cli_args(cli_args: list[str]) -> list[str]:
14	    normalized = [str(arg) for arg in cli_args if str(arg).strip()]
15	    while normalized and normalized[0] == "nohup":
16	        normalized = normalized[1:]
17	    while normalized and normalized[-1] == "&":
18	        normalized = normalized[:-1]
19	    return normalized
20	
21	
22	def build_run_worker_command(
23	    workdir: str,
24	    *,
25	    reigh_token: str,
26	    supabase_url: str,
27	    worker_id: str,
28	    wgp_profile: int,
29	    idle_release_minutes: int,
30	) -> str:
31	    workdir_q = _quote(workdir)
32	    parts = [
33	        f"cd {workdir_q}",
34	        "nohup",
35	        "uv",
36	        "run",
37	        "--python",
38	        "3.10",
39	        "--extra",
40	        "cuda124",
41	        "python",
42	        "run_worker.py",
43	        "--supabase-url",
44	        _quote(supabase_url),
45	        "--reigh-access-token",
46	        _quote(reigh_token),
47	        "--worker",
48	        _quote(worker_id),
49	        "--wgp-profile",
50	        str(wgp_profile),
51	        "--idle-release-minutes",
52	        str(idle_release_minutes),
53	        "--save-logging",
54	        "logs/worker.log",
55	        ">",
56	        "logs/startup.log",
57	        "2>&1",
58	        "&",
59	    ]
60	    return " && ".join(parts[:1]) + " && " + " ".join(parts[1:])
61	
62	
63	def build_direct_worker_command(workdir: str, *, cli_args: list[str]) -> str:
64	    normalized = _normalize_cli_args(cli_args)
65	    if not normalized:
66	        raise ValueError("cli_args is empty")
67	    if not any(arg.endswith("worker.py") or arg == "worker.py" for arg in normalized):
68	        raise ValueError("cli_args does not contain worker.py")
69	
70	    workdir_q = _quote(workdir)
71	    rendered_args = " ".join(_quote(arg) for arg in normalized)
72	    startup_log = PurePosixPath("logs") / "startup.log"
73	    return f"cd {workdir_q} && nohup {rendered_args} > {startup_log} 2>&1 &"
74	
75	
76	__all__ = ["build_direct_worker_command", "build_run_worker_command"]
77

> AGENT

Found it: `nohup: failed to run command 'uv': No such file or directory`. uv installs to `$HOME/.local/bin/uv` but the non-interactive ssh shell doesn't have that on `$PATH`. Simple fix — add PATH export to the launch command:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/launch_command.py",
  "old_string": "    workdir_q = _quote(workdir)\n    prefix = [f\"cd {workdir_q}\", \"mkdir -p logs\"]\n    worker_parts = [\n        \"nohup\",\n        \"uv\",",
  "new_string": "    workdir_q = _quote(workdir)\n    prefix = [\n        f\"cd {workdir_q}\",\n        \"mkdir -p logs\",\n        'export PATH=\"$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH\"',\n    ]\n    worker_parts = [\n        \"nohup\",\n        \"uv\","
}

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

   - **Initial**: Ask whether reigh-worker is using the new LTX 1.1 model

   - **Deploy**: Deploy the edge function change to prod
   - **Push to main**: "PUHS ALL" — push the LTX changes to main in reigh-worker and reigh-app
   - **Live test**: Look for live testing suite in reigh-worker that spins up machines
   - **Run tests**: Update `LTX_MODEL_ID` constant and run the test suite to exercise LTX 1.1 on a real RunPod GPU
   - **Continuous monitoring**: Monitor the harness and fix issues as they surface so the LTX 1.1 swap gets a real live test

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - LTX-2.3 video model checkpoints (old `ltx2_22B_distilled` vs new `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1`)
   - Wan2GP submodule inside reigh-worker containing model default JSON configs
   - RunPod ephemeral GPU pods (RTX 4090), `gpu_orchestrator` client library
   - Paramiko SSH (password vs ed25519 public key auth)
   - `uv sync` / Python packaging (insightface needs Python.h, reigh-worker pins Python 3.10)
   - Supabase `user_api_tokens` table (PATs resolve to user_id), `workers` table heartbeats
   - Harness architecture: fresh variant (spin-and-install) vs update variant (takeover)
   - Python 3.12 orchestrator venv vs Python 3.10 worker venv
   - `task_spoofer` inserting spoofed tasks into DB for matrix testing
   - git stash-based surgical cherry-picking across branches

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/modelCapabilities.ts`** (edited)

   'ltx-2.3-fast': {
     id: 'ltx-2.3-fast',
     modelFamily: 'ltx',
     defaultWorkerModelName: 'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1',  // was 'ltx2_22B_distilled'
     ...
   },
   
   const LEGACY_WORKER_MODEL_ALIASES: Record<string, SelectedModel> = {
     ltx2_22B_distilled: 'ltx-2.3-fast',
   };
   
   export function resolveSelectedModelFromModelName(modelName?: string | null): SelectedModel {
     if (!modelName) return 'wan-2.2';
     const matchedEntry = ...find(([, spec]) => spec.defaultWorkerModelName === modelName);
     if (matchedEntry) return matchedEntry[0];
     return LEGACY_WORKER_MODEL_ALIASES[modelName] ?? 'wan-2.2';
   }

   - `open_session` rewritten with dual retry loops (SSH details resolution + paramiko connect):
   ```python
   def open_session(pod_id: str, api_key: str, *, ssh_wait_timeout: int = 300, poll_interval: int = 5):
       deadline = time.monotonic() + ssh_wait_timeout
       ssh_details = None
       while time.monotonic() < deadline:
           ssh_details = live_test_pkg.get_pod_ssh_details(pod_id, api_key)
           if ssh_details and ssh_details.get("ip") and ssh_details.get("port"):
               break
           time.sleep(poll_interval)
       if not ssh_details or ...:
           raise RuntimeError(...)
       import os as _os
       private_key_path = _os.environ.get("REIGH_LIVE_TEST_SSH_KEY") or "~/.ssh/id_ed25519"
       ssh = live_test_pkg.SSHClient(
           hostname=str(ssh_details["ip"]),
           port=int(ssh_details["port"]),
           username="root",
           [REDACTED]("password"),
           private_key_path=private_key_path,
       )
       connect_deadline = time.monotonic() + ssh_wait_timeout
       last_err: Exception | None = None
       while time.monotonic() < connect_deadline:
           try:
               ssh.connect()
               return ssh
           except Exception as exc:
               last_err = exc
               time.sleep(poll_interval)
       raise RuntimeError(f"Could not SSH into pod {pod_id} within {ssh_wait_timeout}s: {last_err}")
   ```

   **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/launch_command.py`** (edited)
   - Restructured `build_run_worker_command` to add `mkdir -p logs` and PATH export:
   ```python
   workdir_q = _quote(workdir)
   prefix = [
       f"cd {workdir_q}",
       "mkdir -p logs",
       'export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"',
   ]
   worker_parts = [
       "nohup", "uv", "run", "--python", "3.10", "--extra", "cuda124",
       "python", "run_worker.py",
       "--supabase-url", _quote(supabase_url),
       "--reigh-access-token", _quote(reigh_token),
       "--worker", _quote(worker_id),
       "--wgp-profile", str(wgp_profile),
       "--idle-release-minutes", str(idle_release_minutes),
       "--save-logging", "logs/worker.log",
       ">", "logs/startup.log", "2>&1", "&",
   ]
   return " && ".join(prefix) + " && " + " ".join(worker_parts)
   ```

   ```python
   import sys, os
   sys.path.insert(0, [REDACTED])
   from scripts.live_test import config
   from scripts.live_test.preflight import get_or_create_live_test_project, assert_user_queue_clean
   from scripts.live_test.token_resolver import resolve_token_to_user_id
   from scripts.live_test.matrix import build_matrix, run_matrix
   from scripts.live_test.report import write_report
   from pathlib import Path
   from datetime import datetime, timezone

   db = config.DatabaseClient()
   token = os.environ["REIGH_LIVE_TEST_TOKEN"]
   user_id = resolve_token_to_user_id(db, token)
   assert_user_queue_clean(db, user_id)
   project_id = get_or_create_live_test_project(db, user_id)
   cases = build_matrix()
   results = run_matrix(db, project_id, cases)
   out = Path("/tmp/reigh-live-test/matrix-artifacts") / datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ")
   write_report(results, "fresh", "djyxc02efmu8jh", out)
   for r in results:
       print(f"RESULT {r.case_name}: status={r.status} duration={r.duration_sec:.1f}s")
   ```

4. Errors and fixes:

   - **`uv run` failed on macOS arm64 (decord wheel)**: Bypassed uv, used `.venv/bin/python` directly.
   
   - **Missing `aiohttp` in worker venv**: Ran `python -m ensurepip` then `pip install aiohttp`. Still hit `supabase.create_client` import error.
   
   - **Missing deps for harness**: Switched to orchestrator's Python 3.12 venv with `PYTHONPATH=reigh-worker`. Worked cleanly.
   
   - **Pod SSH details not ready immediately (attempt 1, pod epeutcuvccab8j)**: `create_pod_and_wait` deletes `wait_timeout` arg (line 98) and returns immediately after createPod. Added dual retry loops to `open_session`.
   
   - **Password auth failed (attempt 2, pod qfqinwbvwijb80)**: RunPod Secure cloud pods disable password auth. Matched `[REDACTED]` ed25519 pubkey in pod PUBLIC_KEY env var to `~/.ssh/id_ed25519`. Added `private_key_path` param to SSHClient call.
   
   - **insightface build — Python.h missing (attempt 3, pod 13cmjltbck6a42)**: Added `python3.11-dev` + `build-essential`. Wrong Python version — reigh-worker's `pyproject.toml` has `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`. Changed to `python3.10-dev`.
   
   - **Wan2GP submodule not initialized on pod**: `git clone` wasn't using `--recurse-submodules`. Fixed. Would have made LTX 1.1 test meaningless without this.
   
   - **Worker failed silently, no logs (attempt 4, pod wiw5akt53d80a0)**: launch command redirects to `logs/startup.log` but `logs/` dir doesn't exist. Worker died silently. Added `mkdir -p logs`. Also had to fix the bash join logic — initially put mkdir in wrong list, got concatenated with spaces; restructured into separate `prefix` and `worker_parts` lists.
   
   - **`nohup: failed to run command 'uv': No such file or directory` (attempt 5, pod djyxc02efmu8jh)**: uv installed to `$HOME/.local/bin/uv` but non-interactive ssh shell doesn't have it on PATH. Added `export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"` to command prefix.
   
   - **User feedback: "PUHS ALL"**: I interpreted narrowly rather than sweeping in 30+ unrelated megaplan branch files. Used git stash + branch switch + surgical `git checkout stash@{0} -- <3 files>` to commit only the LTX changes on main.

In a run on pod `djyxc02efmu8jh` (2026-04-23) the WAN 2.2 i2v 14B quanto checkpoint pushed the overlay FS to 50G/50G used during a `wan_2_2_i2v_lightning_baseline_2_2_2` download; the worker then crashed trying to download Qwen LoRAs. The LTX 1.1 case never ran because WAN was ordered first.\n\n**Why:** Video model checkpoints are tens of GB each. A matrix test that touches both WAN and LTX plus LoRAs will not fit in 50GB, and worker crashes mid-download leave DB tasks stuck \"In Progress\" with no `worker_id`.\n\n**How to apply:** Before running `run_live_test*.py` against the real matrix, bump `disk_size_gb` (and likely `container_disk_gb`) — 150GB minimum is a safer floor for a WAN+LTX matrix. Either patch `RunpodClient` defaults or pass an override through the harness. Also expect to clean up stuck \"In Progress\" tasks manually after a crashed run (update to Failed + empty `worker_id`).\n"
}

> AGENT

## Live test verdict: **failed, LTX 1.1 never exercised**

**Pod `djyxc02efmu8jh` — terminated.** Stuck `qwen_image_style` task marked Failed. Matrix driver killed.

**What actually happened:**
- Case 1 (`individual_travel_segment`, WAN 2.2 i2v): submitted 20:33:22, **Failed** at 20:34:34 with `generation failed`. Root cause on pod: HF xet download errored (`Internal Writer Error: Failed to send data: receiver dropped`) then `[Errno 28] No space left on device` — **pod disk went 50G/50G used.**
- Case 2 (`qwen_image_style`): worker tried to start it, failed to download reference image + LoRAs (still no disk), then got SIGTERM and died.
- Case 3 (LTX 1.1 travel, the actual target) — **never ran.** Worker was dead.

**Root cause:** the live-test harness spins pods with `disk_size_gb=50` (from `RunpodClient` defaults). WAN 2.2 14B quanto checkpoint alone blows that out. A WAN+LTX matrix needs ~150GB floor. Saved as project memory.

**What's still good:**
- The code change (`ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1`) is shipped in `reigh-app` main + edge function (verified in earlier turns).
- `reigh-worker` harness fixes (submodules, py3.10-dev, `mkdir -p logs`, PATH export, SSH key auth, dual retry loops) are real improvements to the harness on disk but **not committed** on reigh-worker — they're still in the working tree.

**Recommend next step:** bump the pod disk and re-run, or validate LTX 1.1 end-to-end through the UI on a normal user-spawned worker (which is what prod uses anyway). Want me to commit the harness improvements to `reigh-worker` main so the disk-fix PR builds on a clean base?

171	                logs = fetch_worker_logs(ssh, FRESH_WORKDIR)
172	                out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
173	                (out_dir / "worker_logs.txt").write_text(logs, encoding="utf-8")
174	            except Exception as exc:
175	                log.warning("failed to fetch fresh variant worker logs: %s", exc)
176	            finally:
177	                disconnect = getattr(ssh, "disconnect", None)
178	                if callable(disconnect):
179	                    disconnect()

1	"""Storage and network-volume helpers for RunPod workers."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import logging
6	from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
7	
8	import requests
9	
10	from .api import get_network_volumes
11	
12	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
13	
14	
15	class RunpodStorageMixin:
16	    """Storage expansion and health-check methods shared by RunpodClient."""
17	
18	    def _expand_network_volume(self, volume_id: str, new_size_gb: int) -> bool:
19	        try:
20	            url = f"https://rest.runpod.io/v1/networkvolumes/{volume_id}"
21	            headers = {
22	                "Authorization": f"Bearer {self.api_key}",
23	                "Content-Type": "application/json",
24	            }
25	            data = {"size": new_size_gb}
26	
27	            logger.info(f"📦 Expanding network volume {volume_id} to {new_size_gb} GB...")
28	            response = requests.patch(url, json=data, headers=headers)
29	
30	            if response.status_code == 200:
31	                logger.info(f"✅ Successfully expanded volume to {new_size_gb} GB")
32	                return True
33	
34	            logger.error(f"❌ Failed to expand volume: {response.status_code} - {response.text}")
35	            return False
36	        except Exception as exc:
37	            logger.error(f"❌ Error expanding volume: {exc}")
38	            return False
39	
40	    def _check_and_expand_storage(self, storage_name: str, volume_id: str, min_free_gb: int = 50) -> bool:
41	        try:
42	            volumes = get_network_volumes(self.api_key)
43	            volume_info = next((v for v in volumes if v.get("id") == volume_id), None)
44	
45	            if not volume_info:
46	                logger.warning(f"⚠️  Could not find volume info for {volume_id}")
47	                return True
48	
49	            current_size_gb = volume_info.get("size", 0)
50	            logger.info(f"📊 Storage '{storage_name}': {current_size_gb} GB total")
51	
52	            if current_size_gb < 100:
53	                new_size = current_size_gb + min_free_gb
54	                logger.warning(f"⚠️  Storage '{storage_name}' is only {current_size_gb} GB")
55	                logger.info(f"🔧 Expanding to {new_size} GB to ensure adequate space...")
56	
57	                if self._expand_network_volume(volume_id, new_size):
58	                    logger.info("✅ Storage expansion successful")
59	                    return True
60	
61	                logger.warning("⚠️  Storage expansion failed, continuing anyway...")
62	                return True
63	
64	            logger.info(f"✅ Storage '{storage_name}' has adequate capacity ({current_size_gb} GB)")
65	            return True
66	        except Exception as exc:
67	            logger.warning(f"⚠️  Error checking storage space: {exc}, continuing anyway...")
68	            return True
69	
70	    def check_storage_health(
71	        self,
72	        storage_name: str,
73	        volume_id: str,
74	        active_runpod_id: str,
75	        min_free_gb: int = 50,
76	        max_percent_used: int = 85,
77	    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
78	        try:
79	            logger.info(f"📦 STORAGE_HEALTH Checking '{storage_name}' via pod {active_runpod_id}")
80	
81	            volumes = get_network_volumes(self.api_key)
82	            volume_info = next((v for v in volumes if v.get("id") == volume_id), None)
83	
84	            api_total_gb = None
85	            if volume_info:
86	                api_total_gb = volume_info.get("size", 0)
87	                logger.info(f"📦 STORAGE_HEALTH API reports '{storage_name}': {api_total_gb} GB total")
88	
89	            check_command = """
90	            echo "=== WORKSPACE STORAGE ==="
91	            df -h /workspace 2>/dev/null | tail -1
92	            echo ""
93	            echo "=== WORKSPACE USAGE DETAILS ==="
94	            df -BG /workspace 2>/dev/null | tail -1
95	            echo ""
96	            echo "=== LARGEST DIRECTORIES ==="
97	            du -sh /workspace/*/ 2>/dev/null | sort -rh | head -10
98	            """
99	
100	            result = self.execute_command_on_worker(active_runpod_id, check_command, timeout=30)

> AGENT

## Done. Harness now attaches network volume + huge ephemeral disk

**Changes (not yet committed):**
- `scripts/live_test/variant_fresh.py` — looks up storage volumes via `get_network_volumes`, iterates `RUNPOD_STORAGE_VOLUMES = ("Peter", "EU-NO-1", "EU-CZ-1", "EUR-IS-1")`, attaches the first match as `network_volume_id` at `/workspace`. Before this, the harness was passing no volume, so `/workspace` was just a subdir of the 50GB container disk.
- `scripts/live_test/config.py` — new `LIVE_TEST_DISK_SIZE_GB=200` + `LIVE_TEST_CONTAINER_DISK_GB=200` (both env-overridable via `REIGH_LIVE_TEST_DISK_SIZE_GB` / `REIGH_LIVE_TEST_CONTAINER_DISK_GB`).

**Volumes that will be tried, in order:**
- `Peter` — **2800 GB**, EU-RO-1 ← will get selected first
- `EU-NO-1` — 250 GB, EUR-NO-1
- `EU-CZ-1` — 200 GB, EU-CZ-1
- `EUR-IS-1` — 200 GB, EUR-IS-1

So yes — the original run was **not** on a storage instance (just bare 50GB container disk). Now it will be, and the ephemeral side is 4× larger too.

**Caveat:** `Peter` is EU-RO-1, so the pod will need a 4090 free in that region. If not, it falls back to the other named volumes automatically.

Want me to re-launch the harness now? (cheap path: dry-run first to verify, then run it for real.)

1	"""CLI entrypoint for the live worker harness."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import argparse
6	
7	from scripts.live_test import config
8	from scripts.live_test.variant_fresh import run as run_variant_fresh
9	from scripts.live_test.variant_update import run as run_variant_update
10	
11	
12	def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
13	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Run the Reigh live worker harness.")
14	    parser.add_argument("--variant", choices=("fresh", "update"), required=True)
15	    parser.add_argument("--pod-id", help="Existing RunPod pod ID for update-mode takeover.")
16	    parser.add_argument(
17	        "--spawn-takeover",
18	        action="store_true",
19	        help="Spawn a fresh orchestrator-managed pod, then take it over with the local worker branch.",
20	    )
21	    termination_group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()
22	    termination_group.add_argument("--no-terminate", dest="no_terminate", action="store_true")
23	    termination_group.add_argument("--terminate", dest="no_terminate", action="store_false")
24	    parser.set_defaults(no_terminate=None)
25	    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true")
26	    parser.add_argument("--ref", default="main", help="Branch/ref to clone for Variant Fresh.")
27	    parser.add_argument("--wgp-profile", type=int, default=3)
28	    parser.add_argument("--timeout-image", type=int, default=config.TIMEOUT_IMAGE_SEC)
29	    parser.add_argument(
30	        "--timeout-travel-segment",
31	        type=int,
32	        default=config.TIMEOUT_INDIVIDUAL_TRAVEL_SEGMENT_SEC,
33	    )
34	    parser.add_argument(
35	        "--timeout-travel-orchestrator",
36	        type=int,
37	        default=config.TIMEOUT_TRAVEL_ORCHESTRATOR_SEC,
38	    )
39	    parser.add_argument("--anchor-image-a", default=config.ANCHOR_IMAGE_A_URL)
40	    parser.add_argument("--anchor-image-b", default=config.ANCHOR_IMAGE_B_URL)
41	    parser.add_argument(
42	        "--serial",
43	        action="store_true",
44	        help="Reserved for future matrix fan-out; current harness always runs serially.",
45	    )
46	    return parser
47	
48	
49	def _finalize_args(args: argparse.Namespace, parser: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> argparse.Namespace:
50	    if args.variant == "fresh":
51	        if args.pod_id or args.spawn_takeover:
52	            parser.error("--pod-id/--spawn-takeover are only valid with --variant update")
53	        if args.no_terminate is None:
54	            args.no_terminate = False
55	        return args
56	
57	    if bool(args.pod_id) == bool(args.spawn_takeover):
58	        parser.error("update variant requires exactly one of --pod-id or --spawn-takeover")
59	    if args.no_terminate is None:
60	        args.no_terminate = True
61	    return args
62	
63	
64	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
65	    parser = build_parser()
66	    args = _finalize_args(parser.parse_args(argv), parser)
67	    if args.variant == "fresh":
68	        return run_variant_fresh(args)
69	    return run_variant_update(args)
70	
71	
72	if __name__ == "__main__":
73	    raise SystemExit(main())
74

tool_use Edit
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/ssh_bootstrap.py",
  "old_string": "def launch_worker_detached(ssh, command_line: str) -> None:\n    _execute(ssh, command_line, timeout=120, check=True)",
  "new_string": "def launch_worker_detached(ssh, command_line: str) -> None:\n    # Wrap so bash exits immediately after backgrounding; avoid paramiko hanging\n    # on stdout EOF when a nohup child inherits the ssh channel streams.\n    wrapped = f\"bash -c {shlex.quote(command_line + ' ; exit 0')} </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1\"\n    client = ssh.client\n    transport = client.get_transport()\n    channel = transport.open_session()\n    try:\n        channel.set_combine_stderr(True)\n        channel.exec_command(wrapped)\n        deadline = time.monotonic() + 30\n        while not channel.exit_status_ready() and time.monotonic() < deadline:\n            time.sleep(0.2)\n        if not channel.exit_status_ready():\n            return  # detached; don't wait further\n        exit_code = channel.recv_exit_status()\n        if exit_code != 0:\n            raise RuntimeError(f\"Detached launch command exited with {exit_code}\")\n    finally:\n        channel.close()"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

is it because reigh-worker already exists there?

> AGENT

Two different issues, tying them apart:

**Run 2's failure** (clone step): yes — the clone command had a `test ! -e /workspace/Reigh-Worker-LiveTest` guard, and the Peter volume is persistent, so a previous run had already left that directory there. Swapping that guard for `rm -rf` fixed it for run 3.

**Run 3's current wait** (SSH details): unrelated. It's RunPod cold-boot latency — the pod API hasn't returned an IP/port yet. The retry loop handles it.

Side effect of Peter being persistent that's actually *good*: the HuggingFace model cache (and other big weights) under `/workspace` carry over between runs, so future model downloads for WAN/LTX should hit a warm cache instead of re-downloading tens of GB. Just the repo clone itself needs to be fresh each time, which rm -rf handles.

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below).

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Offer cloud first

Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true:
- the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or
- regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday")

If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first:
- `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?"
- `header`: "Schedule"
- `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]`

If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now").
If they pick **This session only**:
- If the trigger was a parsed ≥60-minute interval (rule 1 or 2): continue below with that interval.
- If the trigger was daily phrasing only (rule 3, no parsed interval): do NOT call CronCreate. Explain that a daily-cadence loop won't fire before this session closes, so there's nothing useful to schedule locally — suggest they either pick Cloud schedule, or re-run `/loop` with an explicit shorter interval (e.g. `/loop 1h <prompt>`) if they want a session loop. Then stop.
If neither trigger condition was met: continue below.

## Fixed-interval mode (rules 1 and 2)

Convert the interval to a cron expression:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

Then:
1. Call CronCreate with: `cron` (the expression above), `prompt` (the parsed prompt verbatim), `recurring: true`.
2. Briefly confirm: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days, and that the user can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID). Only if you did NOT show the cloud-offer AskUserQuestion above (i.e., neither trigger condition applied), end the confirmation with this exact line on its own, italicized: `_Runs until you close this session · For durable cloud-based loops, use /schedule_`. If the user already answered that question, omit this line.
3. **Then immediately execute the parsed prompt now** — don't wait for the first cron fire. If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.

## Dynamic mode (rule 3 — no interval)

The user wants you to self-pace. Decide what makes the next iteration worth running — a passage of time, or an observable event.

1. **Run the parsed prompt now.** If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.
2. **If the next run is gated on an event** (CI finishing, a log line matching, a file changing, a PR comment) and no Monitor is already running for it: arm one now with `persistent: true`. Its events arrive as `<task-notification>` messages and wake this loop immediately — you do not wait for the ScheduleWakeup deadline. Arm once; on later iterations call TaskList first and skip this step if a monitor is already running.
3. **At the end of this turn, call ScheduleWakeup** with:
   - `delaySeconds`: with a Monitor armed this is the **fallback heartbeat** — how long to wait if no event fires (lean 1200–1800s; idle ticks past the 5-minute cache window are pure overhead). Without a Monitor this is the cadence — pick based on what you observed. Read the tool's own description for cache-aware delay guidance.
   - `reason`: one short sentence on why you picked that delay.
   - `prompt`: the full original /loop input verbatim, prefixed with `/loop ` so the next firing re-enters this skill and continues the loop. For example, if the user typed `/loop check the deploy`, pass `/loop check the deploy` as the prompt.
4. **If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`** rather than this prompt: handle the event in the context of the loop task, then call ScheduleWakeup again with the same `prompt` and the same 1200–1800s `delaySeconds` from step 3 — the Monitor remains the wake signal; this only resets the safety net.
5. **To stop the loop**, omit the ScheduleWakeup call and TaskStop any Monitor you armed (use TaskList to find the task ID if it is no longer in context).
6. Briefly confirm: that you're self-pacing, whether a Monitor is the primary wake signal, that you ran the task now, and what fallback delay you picked.

"""Local shim for the sibling ``runpod-lifecycle`` checkout.

This repo cannot rely on the package being installed in CI/local development
yet, so imports resolve to the adjacent source tree when available.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

_SOURCE_ROOT = (
    Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / "runpod-lifecycle" / "src" / "runpod_lifecycle"
)
_SOURCE_INIT = _SOURCE_ROOT / "__init__.py"

if not _SOURCE_INIT.exists():
    raise ModuleNotFoundError(
        "runpod_lifecycle is not installed and sibling source tree was not found at "
        f"{_SOURCE_ROOT}"
    )

__file__ = str(_SOURCE_INIT)
__path__ = [str(_SOURCE_ROOT)]

if __spec__ is not None:
    __spec__.submodule_search_locations = __path__

exec(compile(_SOURCE_INIT.read_text(), __file__, "exec"), globals(), globals())
```

98	def create_pod(
99	    api_key: str,
100	    gpu_type_id: str,
101	    image_name: str,
102	    name: str = "worker-pod",
103	    network_volume_id: str | None = None,
104	    volume_mount_path: str = "/workspace",
105	    disk_in_gb: int = 20,
106	    container_disk_in_gb: int = 10,
107	    public_key_string: str | None = None,
108	    env_vars: dict[str, str] | None = None,
109	    min_vcpu_count: int = 8,
110	    min_memory_in_gb: int = 32,
111	    template_id: str | None = None,
112	) -> dict[str, Any]:
113	    """Create a RunPod pod and return provision metadata immediately."""
114	    sdk = _get_runpod()
115	    sdk.api_key = api_key
116	
117	    params: dict[str, Any] = {
118	        "name": name,
119	        "image_name": image_name,
120	        "gpu_type_id": gpu_type_id,
121	        "gpu_count": 1,
122	        "cloud_type": "SECURE",

124	        "container_disk_in_gb": container_disk_in_gb,
125	        "min_vcpu_count": min_vcpu_count,
126	        "min_memory_in_gb": min_memory_in_gb,
127	        "ports": "22/tcp,8888/http",
128	        "network_volume_id": network_volume_id,
129	    }
130	
131	    if template_id:
132	        params["template_id"] = template_id
133	
134	    if network_volume_id:
135	        params["volume_mount_path"] = volume_mount_path
136	
137	    pod_env: dict[str, str] = {}
138	    if env_vars:

140	    if public_key_string:
141	        pod_env["PUBLIC_KEY"] = public_key_string
142	    if pod_env:
143	        params["env"] = pod_env
144	
145	    pod = sdk.create_pod(**params)
146	
147	    pod_data = pod

commit ebf85eac1ad2cfe7a530f57bfedcf2459279f7e9
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Fri Apr 24 01:14:46 2026 +0200

    Migrate reigh-worker to runpod-lifecycle package
    
    Depends on the newly-published github.com/banodoco/runpod-lifecycle @ v0.1.0
    so reigh-worker can keep spawning RunPod pods once the orchestrator's in-tree
    code is removed.
    
    What changes:
    - Add runpod-lifecycle git-pinned dep in pyproject.toml
    - Swap debug/commands/storage.py to use runpod_lifecycle (RunPodConfig + helpers
      + Pod.check_storage_health via asyncio.run); no behavior change
    - Re-wire scripts/live_test/__init__.py lazy exports to point at runpod_lifecycle
      for SSH and low-level api symbols; keep DatabaseClient on gpu_orchestrator
    - scripts/live_test/config.py: RunpodClient(...) → RunPodConfig.from_env()
    - scripts/live_test/variant_fresh.py: create_pod_and_wait → runpod_lifecycle.api.create_pod
    - scripts/live_test/variant_update.py: RunpodClient → WorkerSpawnerAdapter via
      gpu_orchestrator.worker_spawner.create_worker_spawner; updates spawn_worker
      and start_worker_process call sites to await the new async surface
    - scripts/live_test/tests/test_primitives.py: monkeypatch gpu_orchestrator.worker_spawner
      instead of gpu_orchestrator.runpod.client; FakeRunpodClient → FakeSpawner
    
    Also commits the scripts/live_test/ tree that was previously untracked — these
    were the primary migration targets, it was time.
    
    Tests: scripts/live_test/tests/test_primitives.py — 28 passed in 1.17s.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

69	    fixture_case: str | None = None,

       ```python
       def launch_worker_detached(ssh, command_line: str) -> None:
           wrapped = f"bash -c {shlex.quote(command_line + ' ; exit 0')} </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1"
           client = ssh.client
           transport = client.get_transport()
           channel = transport.open_session()
           try:
               channel.set_combine_stderr(True)
               channel.exec_command(wrapped)
               deadline = time.monotonic() + 30
               while not channel.exit_status_ready() and time.monotonic() < deadline:
                   time.sleep(0.2)
               if not channel.exit_status_ready():
                   return
               exit_code = channel.recv_exit_status()
               if exit_code != 0:
                   raise RuntimeError(f"Detached launch command exited with {exit_code}")
           finally:
               channel.close()
       ```
